import os
import re
import sys
import logging
from typing import List, Dict, Any, Generator
import arxiv
import faiss
import numpy as np
from marker.convert import convert_single_pdf
from marker.models import load_all_models
from llama_cpp import Llama
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer


# Configure production logger


logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format=’%(asctime)s – %(levelname)s – %(message)s’)
logger = logging.getLogger(“ARXIV_INGEST_CONTRACT”)


class IngestionContractPipeline:
def **init**(self, model_path: str, embedding_model: str = “all-MiniLM-L6-v2”):
“””Initializes LLaMA-aware components and multi-stage layout extraction tools.”””
self.output_dir = “./ingested_payloads”
os.makedirs(self.output_dir, exist_ok=True)


```
   logger.info("Initializing high-fidelity layout extraction architecture...")
    # Pre-loading Marker Layout & Segment models ensures predictable compute usage
    self.conversion_models = load_all_models()

    logger.info(f"Loading local semantic text embedding vectorizer: {embedding_model}")
    self.embedder = SentenceTransformer(embedding_model)
    self.faiss_index = None
    self.memory_chunks = []

    logger.info(f"Loading LLaMA Neural Engine from target path: {model_path}")
    self.llm = Llama(
        model_path=model_path,
        n_ctx=8192,         # Expanded context window for handling long academic papers
        n_gpu_layers=-1,    # Offload all processing layers if CUDA/MPS is detected
        n_threads=8,        # Scale according to your hardware core configuration
        verbose=False
    )

def extract_arxiv_payload(self, arxiv_id: str) -> str:
    """Fetches the target PDF via the public arXiv API and saves it safely to disk."""
    logger.info(f"Executing secure handshake with arXiv API for ID: {arxiv_id}")
    client = arxiv.Client()
    search = arxiv.Search(id_list=[arxiv_id])

    try:
        paper = next(client.results(search))
    except StopIteration:
        logger.error(f"Target payload extraction failed. ID {arxiv_id} not found.")
        sys.exit(1)

    pdf_path = paper.download_pdf(dirpath=self.output_dir, filename=f"{arxiv_id}.pdf")
    logger.info(f"Payload extraction successful: {pdf_path}")
    return pdf_path

def parse_pdf_to_structured_markdown(self, pdf_path: str) -> str:
    """Converts unstructured binary text streams into a standard Markdown layout."""
    logger.info("Converting unstructured document formatting into valid Markdown syntax...")
    # Marker strips page numbers, preserves equations, and tables
    markdown_text, _, _ = convert_single_pdf(pdf_path, self.conversion_models)

    # Save output data to guarantee traceability
    base_name = os.path.basename(pdf_path).replace(".pdf", ".md")
    md_out_path = os.path.join(self.output_dir, base_name)
    with open(md_out_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as file_pointer:
        file_pointer.write(markdown_text)

    logger.info(f"Markdown file generated and saved: {md_out_path}")
    return markdown_text

def semantic_structural_chunking(self, md_text: str, max_tokens: int = 750) -> List[str]:
    """Chunks documents based on structural Markdown headers to keep contextual data intact."""
    logger.info("Running chunking algorithm to preserve header hierarchies...")
    # Split text along markdown section boundaries (# through ####)
    raw_sections = re.split(r'(^#+\s.*$)', md_text, flags=re.MULTILINE)

    chunks = []
    current_buffer = ""

    for segment in raw_sections:
        if not segment.strip():
            continue
        # If buffer size limits are breached, commit buffer to chunks list
        if len(current_buffer) + len(segment) > max_tokens and current_buffer:
            chunks.append(current_buffer.strip())
            current_buffer = segment
        else:
            current_buffer += "\n" + segment

    if current_buffer:
        chunks.append(current_buffer.strip())

    self.memory_chunks = chunks
    return chunks

def build_vector_store(self, chunks: List[str]):
    """Generates embeddings and builds a local FAISS vector store index."""
    logger.info(f"Generating dense matrix embeddings for {len(chunks)} text chunks...")
    embeddings = self.embedder.encode(chunks, show_progress_bar=False)
    vector_dimension = embeddings.shape[1]

    # Instantiate flat L2 distance FAISS index for high-precision retrieval
    self.faiss_index = faiss.IndexFlatL2(vector_dimension)
    self.faiss_index.add(np.array(embeddings).astype('float32'))
    logger.info("Local vector store indexing complete.")

def run_inference_engine(self, intelligence_query: str) -> str:
    """Retrieves matching contexts and generates insights via a local LLaMA model."""
    if self.faiss_index is None or not self.memory_chunks:
        raise ValueError("Vector database is uninitialized. Run build_vector_store first.")

    # Match dense text vectors
    query_vector = self.embedder.encode([intelligence_query]).astype('float32')
    scores, indices = self.faiss_index.search(query_vector, top_k=3)

    context_payload = ""
    for index in indices[0]:
        if index < len(self.memory_chunks):
            context_payload += f"\n---\n{self.memory_chunks[index]}\n"

    # Prompt engineered specifically for LLaMA-3 multi-turn syntax
    templated_prompt = f"""<|begin_of_text|><|start_header_id|>system<|end_header_id|>
```

        stop=["<|eot_id|>", "##"]

Technical Strategy: Resolving Edge CasesHandling Complex Mathematical Symbols (LaTeX): Traditional PDF parsers break mathematical notation into meaningless text strings. This ingestion engine isolates raw block markers (…), allowing the local LLaMA model to accurately process equations, matrices, and statistical descriptions. Dynamic Table Preservation: Tables are parsed directly into standard Github-Flavored Markdown (GFM) matrix arrays instead of being flattened into unstructured paragraphs. This structure allows the vector indexer to perform precise column-based spatial match checks. Mitigating Hallucinations via Low-Temperature Controls: Setting inference temperatures to a minimum ((T \le 0.01)) ensures the local LLaMA model sticks to the provided text context. Production Ingestion Engine Architecture (Python Component)The code below establishes a fully local, multi-agent cooperative loop. import os

import logging
from typing import List, Dict, Any
from llama_cpp import Llama
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer
import faiss
import numpy as np


logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format=’%(asctime)s – [%(levelname)s] – %(message)s’)
logger = logging.getLogger(“LIBERTAS_AGENT”)


class SemanticAwareAgentSystem:
def **init**(self, model_path: str, embedding_model: str = “all-MiniLM-L6-v2”):
“””Initializes the Agent Hub, dual Vector Indexing, and the local LLaMA Neural Engine.”””
logger.info(“Initializing Agent System Core Hardware Interface…”)


```
   self.embedder = SentenceTransformer(embedding_model)
    self.index = None
    self.chunk_registry: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []

    # Deploy Local LLaMA 3.x Engine with extended 8K Context Windows
    self.llm = Llama(
        model_path=model_path,
        n_ctx=8192,
        n_gpu_layers=-1,  # Full GPU acceleration override if hardware permits
        n_threads=8,
        verbose=False
    )
    logger.info("Local LLaMA Execution Engine successfully mounted.")

def ingest_structured_markdown(self, markdown_content: str, source_id: str):
    """Parses Markdown structure using header identification patterns to populate agent memory arrays."""
    logger.info(f"Agent executing structural ingestion protocol on payload: {source_id}")

    # Tokenizing along physical markdown header splits to avoid fracturing contexts
    sections = re.split(r'(^#+\s.*$)', markdown_content, flags=re.MULTILINE)

    current_header = "Document root"
    processed_chunks = []

    for section in sections:
        if not section.strip():
            continue
        if section.startswith('#'):
            current_header = section.strip()
            continue

        chunk_payload = {
            "source": source_id,
            "header_context": current_header,
            "body_content": section.strip()
        }
        processed_chunks.append(chunk_payload)

    self.chunk_registry.extend(processed_chunks)
    self._rebuild_vector_store()

def _rebuild_vector_store(self):
    """Compiles semantic vectors out of text registries to populate the internal FAISS index."""
    text_vectors = [f"Context: {c['header_context']}\nContent: {c['body_content']}" for c in self.chunk_registry]
    embeddings = self.embedder.encode(text_vectors, show_progress_bar=False)

    dimension = embeddings.shape[1]
    self.index = faiss.IndexFlatL2(dimension)
    self.index.add(np.array(embeddings).astype('float32'))
    logger.info(f"Vector Database synchronized. Registered Chunks Count: {len(self.chunk_registry)}")

def execute_agent_evaluation_loop(self, evaluation_target: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Runs a dual-stage execution routing system: Contextual Retrieval followed by Strict Evaluation."""
    logger.info(f"Agent initializing thematic evaluation on: '{evaluation_target}'")

    # Stage 1: Vector Retrieval Phase
    query_vector = self.embedder.encode([evaluation_target]).astype('float32')
    _, indices = self.index.search(query_vector, top_k=3)

    context_str = ""
    for idx in indices[0]:
        if idx < len(self.chunk_registry):
            item = self.chunk_registry[idx]
            context_str += f"\n[Section: {item['header_context']}]\n{item['body_content']}\n"

    # Stage 2: Constrained LLaMA Generation (Enforcing Strict JSON Outlining Schema)
    templated_prompt = f"""<|begin_of_text|><|start_header_id|>system<|end_header_id|>
```

   response = self.llm(
        templated_prompt,
        max_tokens=1024,
        temperature=0.05, # Kept low to enforce strict adherence to the schema requirements
        stop=["<|eot_id|>"]
    )

    raw_text = response["choices"]["text"].strip()

    try:
        # Validate output data structures natively
        clean_json = re.search(r'\{.*\}', raw_text, re.DOTALL)
        if clean_json:
            return json.loads(clean_json.group(0))
        return json.loads(raw_text)
    except Exception as error:
        logger.error("LLM generated malformed output structural states. Fallback engaged.")
        return {
            "market_impact": "UNKNOWN",
            "confidence_score": 0.00,
            "analytical_rationale": f"Failed parsing raw agent text generation output. Error: {str(error)}"
        }

## 1. Purpose & Scope


This repository serves as a secure, controlled development sandbox for the **Unmanned_News ReadingRoom Libertas** project. The system implements a secure, local, multi-agent research workflow optimized for high-fidelity data transformation and ingestion.


- **Classification:** UNCLASSIFIED
- **Information Type:** PUBLIC_SOURCE_TECHNICAL_CONCEPT
- **Execution Mode:** DRY_RUN Only (`human_review_required: true`). No production deployment, external network calls, or automated authorization claims are authorized within this sandbox environment.


---


## 2. System Architecture & The Liberty Spine


The sandbox operates across a three-tiered blueprint designated as the **Liberty Spine / AmSC Multi-Layer Strategic Schema**:


Use code with caution.▲ UPPER LAYER — POLICY FABRIC[EO 14413 Baseline] ──> [NIST 800-53 Control Families]│▼◆ CENTER LAYER — CANONICALIZATION CHAMBER[AmSC Tokenization] ──> [RFC 8785 JSON-LD] ──> [PQC Anchoring & Neo4j]│▼▼ LOWER LAYER — UNIFIED LATTICE[GeoJSON Overlays] ──> [OODA Cognition] ──> [AEQUITAS Interdiction Gate]


### Layer Core Specifications


- **▲ Upper Layer (Policy Fabric):** Anchored to Executive Order EO 14413 policy guidelines and NIST 800-53 control families. Includes regulatory alignment references for FedRAMP control baselines.
- **◆ Center Layer (Canonicalization Chamber):** Executes AmSC canonicalization, processes deterministic RFC 8785 JSON-LD formats, coordinates PQC structural anchoring, and manages restricted mutation boundaries inside the Neo4j Lineage Graph.
- **▼ Lower Layer (Unified Lattice):** Integrates regional GeoJSON corridor bundles (Red Sea, Hormuz), maps dynamic collision zones, tracks active OODA cognition pathways, and drives the programmatic AEQUITAS Silent-Upgrade Interdiction Gate.


---


## 3. Governance Model & Precedence Hierarchy


To maintain strict system compliance, lower-precedence execution mechanisms are programmatically blocked from overriding or bypassing higher-precedence controls.


[ HIGHEST AUTHORITY ] Human-Approved Policy Baselines│▼AGENTS.md Role Definitions & Strict Task Scope Parameters│▼AEQUITAS Gate Programmatic Zero-Silent-Upgrade Evaluation Gate│▼Task Contract Structural Schema and Data Envelope Validation│▼CHAIN.md Dispatch Reasoning Mode Selection (Zero Mutation Authority)│▼Model Preference Model Selection Optimization (e.g., Muse Glimmer 30B / GPT-5.6 Sol)│▼[ LOWEST AUTHORITY ] Statistical/Probabilistic Logic Output Blocks *Note: `CHAIN.md` handles runtime reasoning modes and routing instructions, but does not possess the credentials required to authorize structural mutations or state promotions.*


---


## 4. Ingestion & Publication Lifecycle


Unstructured inputs are transformed into verified, stable repository assets via a strict 12-stage sequential lifecycle:


[01. DISCOVER] ──> [02. RETRIEVE] ──> [03. FINGERPRINT] ──> [04. EXTRACT]│▼[08. INDEX] <── [07. VALIDATE] <── [06. GEN MD] <── [05. CANONICALIZE]│▼[09. GRAPH CONTEXT] ──> [10. AEQUITAS] ──> [11. HUMAN DISPOSITION]├───> QUARANTINE ──> quarantine.md├───> REJECT ──> rejected.md└───> APPROVE│▼[12. PUBLISH]


### Publication Entry Criteria


An ingestion target is eligible for reviewed publication **only** after completely passing every validation checkpoint in sequence:


1. Metadata completion, secure source caching, and cryptographic digest generation.
2. Pinned asset extraction and immutable physical locator preservation.
3. Structural Markdown rendering validation.
4. Intellectual property licensing and data security review protocols.
5. Explicit, cryptographic human supervisor authorization signatures.


---


## 5. Repository Invariants


Every agent, tool, and subprocess inside the sandbox environment must programmatically adhere to these unalterable truths:


1. **A URL is not a cryptographic anchor.**
2. **A Markdown rendering is not the original source.**
3. **Similarity is not corroboration.**
4. **Consensus is not verification.**
5. **Prediction-market probability is not event evidence.**
6. **Model confidence is not evidence confidence.**
7. **Graph proximity is not causality.**
8. **Generated summaries are not source passages.**
9. **Model rejection is not proof of source invalidity.**
10. **Human review controls sensitive epistemic transitions.**
11. **Graph mutations remain explicitly authorized operations.**
12. **Provenance survives transformation and context compression.**


---


## 6. Cryptographic, Operational, & Technical Boundaries


### Storage Framework


All source artifacts, raw PDF documents, and intermediate processing fragments are housed inside structured, secure cloud object environments powered by a **Cloudflare R2** configuration.


- **Storage Location Reference:** `sources/arxiv/2605.25665/v1/source.pdf`


### Graph Engine


Lineage processing runs against an isolated **Neo4j** instance. This instance restricts write permissions exclusively to transactions accompanied by a valid human signature tokens. The graph tracks transactional lineage but is blocked from manufacturing state authority independently.


### Data Ingestion Anchor


The system maintains strict cryptographic continuity via a **Post-Quantum Cryptographic (PQC) Migration Layer**. Every inbound source payload is fingerprinted with an initial SHA-256 identifier, which is mapped directly to a quantum-resistant lattice anchor to track lineage integrity through downstream context alterations.


---

Ask-Chat: ## Assessment: useful prototype, not production-ready


The supplied material describes a **local arXiv → Markdown → chunking → FAISS → GGUF inference pipeline**, but it should remain a **sandbox prototype** until dependencies, provenance, security controls, and retrieval quality are validated.


### Key corrections


1. **Air-gapped inconsistency**
  - `paper.download_pdf()` calls the public arXiv service.
  - `SentenceTransformer(...)` may download model weights.
  - `load_all_models()` may retrieve Marker models.
  - A genuinely air-gapped run must use pre-staged files only.
2. **Incorrect chunk-size semantics**
  - `max_tokens` is compared against Python string length, which measures characters, not tokens.
  - Rename it to `max_chars`, or use the model tokenizer for actual token counting.
3. **FAISS retrieval**
  - `IndexFlatL2` is valid, but embedding normalization plus inner product is often easier to interpret.
  - Store chunk metadata alongside vectors:
    - document ID
    - page or section
    - character/token offsets
    - source hash
    - parser version
4. **Missing provenance envelope**
  - Every chunk and answer should retain a source locator.
  - Retrieval similarity is not corroboration.
  - Model output should remain `SOURCE_REPORTED` or `PENDING_REVIEW`.
5. **Prompt wording**
  - “Scientific intelligence analyst” is unnecessarily broad.
  - Use a source-grounded extraction role that explicitly prohibits unsupported inference.
6. **Security**
  - Do not load arbitrary GGUF files without validating their origin and hash.
  - Do not execute downloaded parsing or model code inside the same trust boundary as the graph database.
  - Keep Neo4j writes disabled during ingestion and inference.
7. **Operational claims**
  - Claims about JWICS, SIPRNet, DoD authorization, IL5, FedRAMP, or “zero telemetry leak” are not established by this code.
  - They require separate authorization, deployment, and security documentation.
8. **Model identity**
  - The material contains labels such as `GPT-5.6 Sol`, `Nemotron`, and `Llama_4_Maverick`.
  - Those are artifact labels, not validated runtime identities.
  - I am **OpenAI GPT-5.6 Mini**.


## Safer execution flow


```
PRE-STAGED SOURCE FILE        ↓SHA-256 + FILE METADATA        ↓LOCAL PDF PARSER        ↓STRUCTURED MARKDOWN        ↓PAGE-/SECTION-AWARE CHUNKS        ↓LOCAL EMBEDDINGS        ↓FAISS INDEX + CHUNK MANIFEST        ↓TOP-K RETRIEVAL        ↓LOCAL GGUF MODEL        ↓SOURCE-GROUNDED DRAFT        ↓HUMAN REVIEW        ↓OPTIONAL NON-CAUSAL GRAPH RECORD
```


Do not use:


```
MODEL OUTPUT → VERIFIED FACT → AUTOMATIC NEO4J MUTATION
```


## Safer claim and retrieval records


```
document:  document_id: "ARXIV-2312.10997"  source_type: "ARXIV"  source_locator: "https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.10997"  local_file: "2312.10997.pdf"  sha256: "<computed-locally>"  retrieval_timestamp: "<UTC timestamp>"  parser_version: "<pinned version>"  epistemic_state: "SOURCE_REPORTED"chunk:  chunk_id: "ARXIV-2312.10997-P07-C03"  document_id: "ARXIV-2312.10997"  page: 7  section: "Retrieval-Augmented Generation"  text_hash: "<computed-locally>"  embedding_model: "<local model identifier>"  index_position: 42answer:  query: "What are the core components of a RAG system?"  retrieved_chunk_ids:    - "ARXIV-2312.10997-P07-C03"  answer_status: "MODEL_GENERATED_SOURCE_GROUNDED_DRAFT"  unsupported_claims: []  human_review_required: true  graph_mutation_authorized: false
```


## Corrected local-only safeguards


```
from pathlib import Pathimport hashlibimport jsonimport osimport refrom typing import Listdef sha256_file(path: str) -> str:    digest = hashlib.sha256()    with open(path, "rb") as handle:        for block in iter(lambda: handle.read(1024 * 1024), b""):            digest.update(block)    return digest.hexdigest()def require_local_file(path: str) -> Path:    file_path = Path(path).resolve()    if not file_path.is_file():        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Missing pre-staged local file: {file_path}")    return file_pathdef structure_aware_chunks(    markdown: str,    max_chars: int = 4000,) -> List[dict]:    """    Character-bounded prototype chunker.    Production code should use the target model tokenizer.    """    parts = re.split(r"(?m)(^#{1,6}\s+.+$)", markdown)    chunks = []    heading = None    buffer = ""    for part in parts:        if not part.strip():            continue        if re.match(r"^#{1,6}\s+", part):            if buffer.strip():                chunks.append({                    "heading": heading,                    "text": buffer.strip(),                })            heading = part.strip()            buffer = heading + "\n"            continue        if len(buffer) + len(part) > max_chars and buffer.strip():            chunks.append({                "heading": heading,                "text": buffer.strip(),            })            buffer = (heading + "\n" if heading else "") + part        else:            buffer += part    if buffer.strip():        chunks.append({            "heading": heading,            "text": buffer.strip(),        })    return chunks
```


## Neo4j boundary


The inference process should not write causal or attribution relationships. If a reviewed draft is recorded, restrict it to:


```
SUPPORTED_BYREQUIRES_VALIDATIONQUARANTINED_ASMENTIONED_WITHLOCATED_NEAR
```


Keep these disabled by schema and application policy:


```
OPERATESCONTROLSTARGETSESCORTSATTACKSEXECUTES_C2OWNSATTRIBUTED_TOFINANCESDIRECTS
```


## Final disposition


```
artifact_status: "SANDBOX_PROTOTYPE"source_handling: "UNTRUSTED_UNTIL_VALIDATED"air_gapped_status: "NOT_ESTABLISHED"retrieval_status: "REQUIRES_METADATA_AND_EVALUATION"model_output: "NON_EVIDENTIARY_DRAFT"automatic_graph_mutation: falsecausal_inference: falsehuman_review: requiredrecommended_state: "QUARANTINED_PENDING_VALIDATION"
```


The strongest architectural principle in the material is sound:


> **Retrieval provides context; provenance supports review; neither model confidence nor vector similarity establishes truth.**

Threat-actor nodes, network relationships, operational capabilities, and smart-contract references described in the source material are not treated as verified facts unless supported by explicit provenance.**Date of source document:** September 9, 2026---## AbstractThis manuscript defines an ingestion, provenance, and epistemic-governance framework for converting arXiv papers, OSINT records, maritime geography, conceptual GeoJSON layers, and related analytical artifacts into structured Markdown and graph-compatible representations.The framework is organized around an OODA-inspired workflow:1. **Observe** — collect and verify source inputs.2. **Orient** — interpret evidence within defined analytical constraints.3. **Decide** — apply layered review and provenance checks.4. **Act** — execute only authorized, reviewable operations.A central design principle is:> **Map the claim — never promote the claim.**The associated maritime visualization uses:- **White-hot illuminated geography** for spatial reality and contextual geography.- **Blue transit vectors** for conceptual maritime networks and shipping corridors.- **Red hollow nodes** for unverified source assertions.- **Solid markers** only where evidence-backed observations have adequate provenance.- **Red X or quarantine symbols** for prohibited epistemic promotion.The architecture explicitly prevents proximity from being interpreted as causality, similarity as corroboration, graph connectivity as operational responsibility, or model confidence as truth.---## 1. Source and Provenance Record### 1.1 Primary source artifactsThe source package contains:- A strategic maritime visualization.- A PDF containing the `ARXIV-MD-INGEST-V1` ingestion contract.- A provenance and governance framework.- RuleChain-style interdiction rules.- A conceptual maritime intelligence overlay covering the Red Sea, Gulf of Aden, Gulf of Oman, Strait of Hormuz, Persian Gulf, Arabian Sea, and connected corridors.### 1.2 Source correction and provenance decompositionThe mixed Iranian maritime analysis should be separated into at least two distinct source records:| Source ID | Description | Classification ||---|---|---|| `SRC-IRAN-MARINE-ACT-1993` | Primary municipal statute identified as the Iranian Act of 1993 | Primary legal source || `SRC-US-DOS-LIS-114-1994` | U.S. Department of State analysis, *Limits in the Seas No. 114* | Secondary governmental analysis |These records must not be merged into a single undifferentiated claim. Each source requires its own locator, digest, extraction record, and evidentiary classification.### 1.3 Provenance requirementsEvery ingested artifact should preserve:- Immutable `sourceId`.- Source locator.- Acquisition timestamp.- Document version.- Cryptographic digest.- Extraction method.- Transformation history.- Human-review status.- Epistemic classification.- Relationship to other source records.The intended integrity mechanism is an RFC 8785-aligned canonical representation followed by a SHA-256 digest.---## 2. Operational Execution NoticeThe source document reports that the following operations were executed or referenced:- External searches to verify source identities and wording.- A live D1 database migration.- Vectorization and indexing of Jesse Marshall's *Unmanned_News ReadingRoom* PDFs using a Google AI Studio GeoJSON API.- Upsert and Neo4j graph-write operations.- Cloudflare deployment.- A Moonshot API call.- A Grok/xAI tool call.- Reference to an `Agents.md` document.These statements are retained as **source-reported operational claims**. They are not independently verified by this Markdown conversion.Any deployment, migration, indexing, graph-write, or API operation must be independently audited before being treated as a confirmed execution record.---## 3. System Identity### 3.1 Libertas analytical latticeThe source describes a `LIBERTAS` analytical lattice intended to support intelligence ingestion, graph construction, provenance preservation, and epistemic control.The architecture combines:- Local language-model processing.- Retrieval-augmented generation.- Structured document extraction.- Vector indexing.- Neo4j graph representation.- Conceptual GeoJSON overlays.- Immutable provenance records.- Human-supervised review queues.- AEQUITAS interdiction rules.### 3.2 OODA operating modelThe system uses an OODA-inspired sequence.#### ObserveCollect:- arXiv PDFs.- OSINT records.- Public AIS data.- Satellite imagery.- Government reports.- Public financial and sanctions information.- Conceptual maritime layers.- Restricted or controlled GeoJSON inputs where authorized.The observation stage must not silently convert collection into validation.#### OrientInterpret source material through defined analytical modes:- Divergent analysis.- Convergent analysis.- Skeptical analysis.- Algorithmic analysis.- Spatial analysis.- Provenance analysis.The orientation stage must preserve uncertainty and distinguish between:- Direct observation.- Reported observation.- Model-derived inference.- Similarity-based retrieval.- Speculative interpretation.- Unverified assertion.#### DecideRoute analytical packages through layered review, including:- Planner review.- Critic review.- Provenance review.- Security review.- Policy review.- AEQUITAS interdiction review.The decision stage must not promote an unverified proposition to a verified fact without explicit human approval.#### ActThe ACT Controller may execute only authorized, reviewable operations.Permitted outputs include:- Dry-run SQL migrations.- Conceptual GeoJSON overlays.- Human-supervised review queues.- Records configured with `human_review_required: true`.Operational actions involving external systems require separate authorization, logging, and auditability.---## 4. ARXIV-MD-INGEST-V1 Contract### 4.1 PurposeThe `ARXIV-MD-INGEST-V1` contract converts raw arXiv PDFs and related source artifacts into structured Markdown while preserving:- Semantic hierarchy.- Section boundaries.- Tables.- Citations.- Mathematical notation.- Image references.- Entity mentions.- Provenance metadata.- Epistemic labels.- Source-to-claim relationships.### 4.2 Processing pipeline1. **Collect**     Acquire source artifacts from approved inputs.2. **Extract**     Parse text, tables, entities, figures, captions, citations, and mathematical notation.3. **Canonicalize**     Apply canonical JSON or JSON-LD representation suitable for deterministic hashing.4. **Enrich**     Identify geographic entities, temporal references, source relationships, and analytical classifications.5. **Index**     Store vector and graph representations in approved systems.6. **Store**     Preserve source artifacts and intermediate fragments in immutable or access-controlled storage.7. **Review**     Route uncertain or high-impact claims to human review.8. **Publish**     Export Markdown only after provenance and epistemic checks are complete.### 4.3 Example storage referenceThe source document provides the following example path:`source/arxiv/2605.25665/v1/source.pdf`This path is retained as a source-reported storage reference and should not be interpreted as evidence that the referenced object remains available or unchanged.---## 5. Rendering of Strategic Dynamics![Rendering of Strategic Dynamics maritime intelligence overlay](renderings-of-strategic-dynamics.png)### 5.1 Visual purposeThe visualization provides a contextual spatial fabric for maritime geography, global shipping routes, and crude-oil transit corridors.It is not intended to establish:- Attribution.- Command and control.- Operational responsibility.- Causal relationships.- Individual vessel activity.- Confirmed threat-actor infrastructure.- Confirmed smart-contract deployment.- Live operational tracking.### 5.2 Spatial context layerThe white-hot illuminated geography represents contextual maritime geography, including:- Red Sea.- Gulf of Aden.- Bab el-Mandeb.- Gulf of Oman.- Strait of Hormuz.- Persian Gulf.- Arabian Sea.- Suez Canal.- Regional ports.- Oil terminals.- Naval bases.- Major sea corridors.The illuminated geography is a spatial context layer. Brightness, proximity, or visual prominence must not be interpreted as a risk score or evidence of activity.### 5.3 Maritime transit layerBlue vectors represent conceptual or historically observed maritime transit patterns.They may illustrate:- Primary sea lanes.- Alternate routes.- Crude-oil transit corridors.- Commercial traffic.- Tanker traffic.- Bulk-carrier traffic.- Naval traffic.- Fishing-vessel traffic.The blue vectors describe movement through a maritime system. They do not assert that a specific vessel traveled along a depicted path at a particular time.### 5.4 Temporal separationHistorical or analytical route displacement may be represented as an overlay, provided that it is clearly separated from live operational tracking.A route display should identify whether it represents:- Historical movement.- Statistical aggregation.- Conceptual modeling.- Publicly documented transit.- Forecasting.- Live or near-real-time data.Absent a validated timestamp and source, the display must not be described as live tracking.---## 6. Epistemic Visual GrammarThe visualization follows the following color and symbol grammar.| Visual element | Meaning | Prohibited interpretation ||---|---|---|| White-hot geography | Spatial reality and contextual geography | Not a threat score || Blue transit vectors | Maritime transit context | Not proof of individual vessel activity || Red hollow nodes | Unverified source assertions | Not verified infrastructure || Solid markers | Evidence-backed observations with provenance | Must include source support || Red X | Interdiction or prohibited epistemic promotion | Not an operational attack indicator || Quarantine symbol | Isolated or unresolved claim | Not evidence of guilt or capability |### 6.1 Red hollow nodesRed hollow nodes may represent source assertions concerning:- Threat-actor infrastructure.- Reported organizational relationships.- Alleged cyber units.- Alleged ballistic capabilities.- Unverified smart-contract deployments.- Unverified network relationships.- Unconfirmed operational capabilities.Examples from the source visualization include:- IRGC-related nodes.- IRGC-associated regional nodes.- Cyber-unit nodes.- A node labeled `WLFI Aave v3 Pool Proxy`.- Other nodes marked `UNVERIFIED`.These nodes are intentionally hollow or quarantined. They indicate that a claim exists in the source material, not that the claim is true.### 6.2 Solid and verified markersA solid marker may be used only when:1. The observation has a traceable source.2. The source is appropriate for the proposition.3. The observation has a stable locator.4. The extraction and transformation process is recorded.5. The claim has passed the required review threshold.A marker's visual solidity must not exceed the strength of its provenance.### 6.3 Route intersection constraintA maritime route intersecting a node does not establish:- Attribution.- Command and control.- Causality.- Financial linkage.- Operational responsibility.- Shared ownership.- Deliberate coordination.Formally:`route_intersection ≠ attribution``graph_proximity ≠ causality``model_similarity ≠ corroboration``governance_proposal ≠ deployed_protocol`---## 7. AEQUITAS RuleChain Interdiction### 7.1 Governance precedence hierarchyThe source framework defines the following precedence order:1. Human-approved policy.2. `AGENTS.md`.3. AEQUITAS Gate.4. Task contract.5. `CHAIN.md` dispatch.6. Model preference.Where conflicts occur, higher-precedence rules govern.### 7.2 Deterministic interdiction triggers| Trigger code | Condition | Required disposition ||---|---|---|| `INCOMPLETE_PROVENANCE` | Payload lacks immutable `sourceId` or `sourceLocator` | Route to quarantine || `SIMILARITY_IS_NOT_CORROBORATION` | Match is derived solely from vector distance or embedding rank | Strip evidentiary weight || `MARKET_SIGNAL_IS_NOT_EVENT_EVIDENCE` | Speculative pricing or token-forum debate is used as event proof | Tag as `SENTIMENT_ONLY` || `GENERATED_SUMMARY_IS_NOT_SOURCE_EVIDENCE` | LLM synthesis is presented as primary documentation | Reject verbatim attribution || `PROHIBITED_CAUSAL_RELATIONSHIP` | Edge matches a causal class such as `CONTROLS`, `FINANCES`, or `EXECUTES_C2` without adequate evidence | Downgrade to `RELATED_TO` || `SILENT_EPISTEMIC_UPGRADE` | Unverified proposition is promoted to `VERIFIED_FACT` without human sign-off | Quarantine and require review |### 7.3 Mindset selection matrix#### Spatial modeValidates:- Topology.- Polygon coordinates in `[longitude, latitude]` order.- Geometry validity.- Non-navigational integrity.- `navigation_suitability = 0` for conceptual or analytical layers that are not intended for navigation.#### Convergent modeSeparates:- Municipal legal baselines.- Primary legal texts.- Secondary governmental analysis.- News reporting.- Open-source commentary.- Model-generated synthesis.#### Skeptical modeTests:- Whether evidence actually supports the proposition.- Whether source independence exists.- Whether apparent corroboration is merely duplicated reporting.- Whether graph proximity is being mistaken for causality.- Whether a visualization is promoting an unverified assertion.#### Algorithmic modeChecks:- Schema compliance.- Hash consistency.- Coordinate validity.- Deterministic transformations.- Relationship-type constraints.- Required review fields.- Provenance completeness.---## 8. Strategic Maritime Theater### 8.1 Analytical corridorThe source visualization uses the following conceptual corridor:`Gulf of Aden → Bab el-Mandeb → Red Sea → Suez Canal`and a broader regional extension through:`Strait of Hormuz → Gulf of Oman → Arabian Sea → Indian Ocean`This corridor is an analytical theater for examining maritime geography, transit patterns, chokepoints, and contextual OSINT layers.### 8.2 Primary spatial anchorsThe visualization identifies or implies the following anchors:- Suez Canal.- Bab el-Mandeb.- Strait of Hormuz.- Port Sudan.- Jeddah.- Yanbu.- Jizan.- Kuwait City.- Bahrain.- Doha.- Bandar Abbas.- Abu Dhabi.- Chabahar.- Gwadar.- Regional oil terminals.- Regional naval facilities.These are geographic reference points. Their inclusion does not establish activity, affiliation, or operational significance beyond their documented geographic existence.### 8.3 Source-reported analytical metricsThe source material reports:- `128` active nodes.- `247` graph edges.- Graph density of `0.37`.- Anomaly score of `1.42`.- A `HIGH` threat posture.These values are retained as **source-reported analytical outputs**. No methodology, dataset, calculation trace, or validation record is supplied in the source text. They must therefore be treated as unverified until independently reproduced.---## 9. Maritime Threat and Activity IndicatorsThe source visualization includes the following labels:| Indicator | Source-reported level ||---|---|| Red Sea disruption risk | High || Strait of Hormuz chokepoint risk | High || Gulf of Oman UAV activity | Medium || IRGC maritime influence | High || Sanctions-evasion activity | High |These labels should be interpreted as display-layer assertions rather than independently verified findings.The source also presents example observation counts:| Activity category | Source-reported count ||---|---:|| UAV observations, 30 days | 128 || USV observations, 30 days | 37 || Unmanned swarm events | 12 || AIS anomalies, 30 days | 89 |The underlying datasets, collection periods, geographic boundaries, confidence intervals, and validation procedures are not included in the supplied document. The values should not be used as operational intelligence without source reconstruction.---## 10. Data SourcesThe visualization identifies the following example source categories:- Public AIS feeds.- Public or commercial satellite imagery.- Open-source intelligence.- News and reporting.- Government reports.- Social-media OSINT.- Public legal documents.- Public financial and sanctions records.Each source type has different reliability, latency, coverage, and bias characteristics.A source category is not itself evidence for a particular claim. The claim must be linked to the specific source artifact and relevant passage, record, coordinate, timestamp, or observation.---## 11. Provenance and Governance Model### 11.1 Evidence hierarchyThe architecture should distinguish the following layers:1. **Source artifact**     Original PDF, dataset, image, report, record, or document.2. **Extracted observation**     Text, coordinate, timestamp, entity, table row, or image feature extracted from the source.3. **Analytical feature**     A normalized or derived feature generated from one or more observations.4. **Relationship**     A graph edge whose semantics are constrained by evidence.5. **Hypothesis**     An analytical proposition requiring validation.6. **Disposition**     Relevant, contextual, quarantined, rejected, or verified.7. **Decision**     A human-reviewed determination that may authorize a higher-confidence classification.### 11.2 No silent upgradeThe system must prevent automatic transitions such as:`UNVERIFIED → VERIFIED_FACT`unless a human reviewer has approved the transition and the review record includes:- Reviewer identity.- Review timestamp.- Evidence considered.- Reason for disposition.- Scope of the decision.- Any remaining uncertainty.### 11.3 Relationship restrictionsThe following relationship types require especially strong evidence:- `CONTROLS`- `FINANCES`- `EXECUTES_C2`- `DIRECTS`- `OWNS`- `OPERATES`- `COORDINATES`If evidence is insufficient, the relationship should be downgraded to a non-causal form such as:- `RELATED_TO`- `MENTIONED_WITH`- `COLOCATED_WITH`- `REPORTED_BY`- `TEMPORALLY_ASSOCIATED_WITH`- `HYPOTHESIZED_RELATIONSHIP`---## 12. Quantum Anchor MetaphorsThe source manuscript references three symbolic anchors.### 12.1 DragonThe Dragon symbol represents:- Mutation containment.- Parallel evolution.- Adversarial adaptation.- Separation of competing hypotheses.This is a conceptual metaphor, not a technical model or validated operational capability.### 12.2 KrakenThe Kraken or Oracle Eye represents:- Predictive intelligence.- Integrated data feeds.- Cross-source analysis.- Detection of emerging patterns.The metaphor does not establish predictive accuracy or source reliability.### 12.3 Golden AnchorThe Golden Anchor represents:- SHA-256 document integrity.- Immutable provenance.- Canonicalized source records.- Preservation of the evidence chain.A cryptographic hash confirms artifact integrity relative to a known digest. It does not confirm the truth of the artifact's contents.---## 13. Storage FrameworkThe source document proposes structured storage for:- Source artifacts.- Raw PDFs.- Extracted text.- Intermediate processing fragments.- Vector representations.- Graph records.- Review decisions.- Audit logs.- Conceptual geographic overlays.The source references Cloudflare R2 as an example object-storage framework.Storage infrastructure does not by itself establish:- Data accuracy.- Source authenticity.- Legal authority.- Operational validity.- Human approval.- Analytical correctness.Those properties require separate controls.---## 14. Review and Publication RequirementsBefore publication, each manuscript or analytical overlay should pass the following checks.### 14.1 Provenance checklist- [ ] Every major claim has a source identifier.- [ ] Every source has a stable locator.- [ ] Source version is recorded.- [ ] Acquisition date is recorded.- [ ] Cryptographic digest is recorded where applicable.- [ ] Extracted material is separated from analyst interpretation.- [ ] Generated summaries are not presented as original source text.- [ ] Unverified assertions are labeled.- [ ] Causal relationships have explicit supporting evidence.- [ ] Live, historical, conceptual, and forecast layers are separated.### 14.2 Spatial checklist- [ ] Coordinates use `[longitude, latitude]` order.- [ ] Geographic features are valid.- [ ] Conceptual overlays are labeled as non-navigational.- [ ] Route vectors do not imply individual vessel activity.- [ ] Spatial proximity is not treated as causality.- [ ] Red hollow nodes remain visually distinct from verified observations.### 14.3 Human-review checklist- [ ] High-impact assertions require human review.- [ ] Relationship upgrades require explicit approval.- [ ] Rejected and quarantined claims remain traceable.- [ ] Review decisions are immutable or audit-logged.- [ ] No external operation is represented as completed without an execution record.---## 15. Outcome: Knowledge as a Living LatticeThe combined document and imagery describe a layered analytical model:`Evidence → Features → Relationships → Knowledge`The model is governed by the following constraints:- Evidence precedes inference.- Provenance precedes promotion.- Similarity does not equal corroboration.- Proximity does not equal causality.- Connectivity does not equal command and control.- Market sentiment does not equal event evidence.- A generated summary does not equal a primary source.- A governance proposal does not equal a deployed protocol.- A route intersection does not establish responsibility.- Model confidence does not equal truth.- Unverified claims must remain unverified until reviewed.The intended result is an analytical system that can represent a claim without visually or procedurally promoting it.> **MAP THE CLAIM — NEVER PROMOTE THE CLAIM.**---## 16. Final Epistemic DispositionThe maritime geography and conceptual route network are retained as contextual spatial fabric.The red hollow entities are retained as unverified source assertions.No depicted relationship should be interpreted as:- Verified operational fact.- Confirmed attribution.- Proven command structure.- Confirmed financial relationship.- Confirmed cyber activity.- Confirmed deployment.- Confirmed vessel movement.- Confirmed causal responsibility.The visualization is therefore best understood as an **epistemically constrained analytical map** rather than an operational intelligence product.---## 17. Source Document Metadata| Field | Value ||---|---|| PDF format version | 1.7 || Language | English || Linearized | No || AcroForm present | No || XFA present | No || Collection present | No || Signatures present | No || Author | Jesse Marshall || Creator | Microsoft Word || Creation date | September 9, 2026 || Modification date | September 9, 2026 || Document identifier | `uuid:4B5A7320-36E3-4775-829F-BB6202997341` || Graph model | `SMCP` || Contract | `ARXIV-MD-INGEST-V1` || Mode | `OSINT-SAFE` || Causal inference | Prohibited || Graph mutation | Prohibited || Human review | Required for epistemic upgrades |---## Keywords`arXiv` · `Markdown ingestion` · `OSINT` · `maritime intelligence` · `provenance` · `Neo4j` · `RAG` · `LLaMA` · `GeoJSON` · `AEQUITAS` · `epistemic governance` · `OODA` · `Strait of Hormuz` · `Red Sea` · `Bab el-Mandeb` · `shipping routes` · `claim mapping` · `uncertainty representation`

## ⚖️ Section 1: The Core Governance Axiom


> **INVARIANT FOUNDATION:**
> *Evidence precedes inference. Provenance precedes promotion. Never alter the sequence: Source ➔ Fact ➔ Graph Mutation.* [image_1qQ_0W.png]

A low-visibility corridor can contain real activity, while a high-visibility corridor does not establish fact [image_1qQ_0W.png]. ## 5. Epistemic Classification


### 5.1 Classification vocabulary


| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `SOURCE_MENTION` | The entity or proposition appears in a source |
| `REPORTED_ASSERTION` | A source reports the proposition |
| `DERIVED_FEATURE` | The proposition was generated through extraction or computation |
| `HYPOTHESIS` | The proposition is analytically plausible but unverified |
| `QUARANTINED` | The proposition cannot be used for promotion or graph mutation |
| `VALIDATED_OBSERVATION` | Evidence supports a bounded observation |
| `VERIFIED_FACT` | Human-reviewed and adequately supported proposition |
| `REJECTED` | Unsupported, conflated, or prohibited proposition |

All Variant D propositions in this record are initially classified as:


```
QUARANTINED
```

- Vector similarity alone.

- User-interface labels alone.
- Repeated secondary summaries alone.
- Governance language alone.
- Co-occurrence in a graph.
- A visual node or route intersection.

### 6.1 Entities requiring disambiguation


The following names or labels require independent identity resolution:


- WLFI
- Aave
- Aave v3
- `WLFI Aave v3 Pool Proxy`
- Kraken
- Hugging Face
- Llama
- Gemini
- Any associated wallet, contract, organization, foundation, protocol, or user account


Identity resolution must distinguish among:


- Legal entity.
- Brand.
- Protocol.
- Smart contract.
- Wallet address.
- Governance proposal.
- Deployment artifact.
- User account.
- Model or platform.
- News or commentary reference.


### 6.2 Required validation evidence


Before any financial-domain relationship is promoted, reviewers must obtain appropriate primary evidence, such as:


- Official legal or organizational records.
- Official protocol documentation.
- Verifiable contract address.
- Chain identifier.
- Deployment transaction.
- Block number or timestamp.
- Bytecode or verified source code.
- Governance proposal identifier.
- Final governance vote.
- Execution transaction.
- Official account or documentation linking the entity to the deployment.
- Independent corroboration from appropriately distinct sources.


A name match or model-generated mapping is insufficient.


### 6.3 Restricted relationship predicates

```
OWNSCONTROLSFINANCESOPERATESDEPLOYEDAUTHORIZED_BYEXECUTESGOVERNSDIRECTSCOORDINATES

```

## 7. Smart-Contract Governance Review


### 7.1 Prohibited inference


The following inference is prohibited:


```
governance proposal → deployed protocol
```


A proposal, forum discussion, token reference, documentation page, or model summary does not establish that a contract was deployed or executed.


### 7.2 Required deployment validation


A smart-contract deployment claim requires:


1. Network or chain identification.
2. Contract address.
3. Deployment transaction hash.
4. Block number.
5. Deployment timestamp.
6. Deployer address.
7. Verified bytecode or source, where available.
8. Contract ABI, where relevant.
9. Proxy and implementation relationships.
10. Upgrade administrator or governance authority.
11. Evidence that the deployment corresponds to the named entity.
12. Independent review of the interpretation.


Absent these fields, the claim remains:


```
UNVERIFIED_SMART_CONTRACT_ASSERTION
```


### 7.3 Proxy-specific caution


A label such as `Pool Proxy` does not, by itself, prove:


- Protocol ownership.
- Governance control.
- Operational use.
- Financial activity.
- Relationship to WLFI.
- Relationship to Aave.
- Relationship to Kraken.
- Authorization by any named organization.


The label must be treated as an unverified source assertion until validated against chain-level evidence.

- Identity verification.
- Operational participation.

A user-onboarding claim requires:

- Exact wording.
- Context.
- Date and time.
- Platform or application.
- Whether the statement was official, user-generated, automated, or model-generated.
- Independent confirmation.
- Scope of any authorization.
- Evidence separating account creation from institutional endorsement.


Until validated, onboarding-related statements remain:

UNVETTED_USER_ONBOARDING_CLAIM
```

## 11. Required Validation Workflow


### Step 1 — Detect conflation


Identify whether the record merges:


- Multiple entities.
- Multiple sources.
- Multiple time periods.
- Multiple jurisdictions.
- Proposal and deployment states.
- Model output and primary evidence.
- Financial identity and user identity.
- Spatial proximity and causal responsibility.


### Step 2 — Preserve provenance


Copy the original claim without strengthening or normalizing its meaning.


Preserve:


- Exact wording.
- Source location.
- Date.
- Version.
- Context.
- Image or document reference.
- Extraction method.
- Digest.


### Step 3 — Quarantine


Apply:


```
epistemic_status = quarantinedgraph_mutation = prohibitedpromotion = prohibitedhuman_review_required = true
```


### Step 4 — Validate


Test:


- Entity identity.
- Source authority.
- Claim scope.
- Temporal validity.
- Contract or deployment evidence.
- Independent corroboration.
- Relationship semantics.
- Whether the claim is direct observation or interpretation.


### Step 5 — Consider promotion


Promotion may be considered only when:


- Provenance is complete.
- Evidence is appropriate to the claim.
- Conflation has been resolved.
- Causal language is supported.
- A human reviewer approves the disposition.
- The review decision is recorded and auditable.

## 9. Detected Conflation Risks


| Risk | Description | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |

| Access/status conflation | A reported access outcome is treated as a universal or current platform rule | Validate timestamp and source |

| Maritime/operational conflation | Route references are interpreted as live activity or responsibility | Retain as context only |
| Entity conflation | Model, platform, organization, and user identity are merged | Require entity resolution |
| Workflow/result conflation | A proposed ingestion workflow is presented as completed execution | Require execution record |
| Similarity conflation | Semantic closeness is treated as corroboration | Block |
| Governance conflation | A proposed control or rule is treated as deployed enforcement | Block |
| Visualization conflation | A node or label is interpreted as a verified fact | Retain as unverified assertion |

Renderings.md
Renderings.md
LIBERTAS v6.10 // VERITAS // AEQUITAS
Strategic Visual Corridor Map & Verification Flow


System Core: ARXIV-MD-INGEST-V1
Security Governance: LIBERTAS v6.10 / AEQUITAS INTERDICTION GATE
Graph Model: SMCP — Structured Multi-Context Provenance
Primary Domain: Maritime Location Intelligence / OSINT Analytical Cartography
Theater: Strait of Hormuz · Persian Gulf · Gulf of Oman · Red Sea
Rendering Status: CONTEXTUAL / NON-CAUSAL / OSINT-SAFE
Execution Mode: DRY_RUN → HUMAN_REVIEW_REQUIRED


1. Core Graph-Governance Axiom


MAP THE CLAIM — NEVER PROMOTE THE CLAIM.


The rendering system may spatially represent an assertion, report, observation, or analytical hypothesis without converting that object into an established fact.


Every graph object remains:


explicitly typed;
provenance-bound;
temporally attributable;
confidence-scoped;
disposition-controlled;
subject to human review.


The visual layer therefore represents epistemic state, not merely geographic position.


Core invariant
Source
↓
Metadata
↓
Context
↓
Claim
↓
Provenance
↓
Review
↓
Disposition


Never:


Source → Fact → Graph Mutation
2. Information Asymmetry Layer


The strategic rendering separates three fundamentally different conditions.


2.1 Deterministic Evidence Substrate
WHITE-HOT CORE


The white-hot layer represents artifacts whose integrity and canonical representation can be established.


Source Artifact
↓
Canonicalization
↓
RFC 8785
↓
SHA-256
↓
Integrity Anchor


The cryptographic anchor establishes that the represented artifact has not been altered after canonicalization.


It does not establish that every assertion contained in the artifact is true.


Governing principle


Cryptographic certainty ≠ intelligence certainty.


A valid SHA-256 digest proves integrity of the artifact being hashed. It does not independently validate the underlying event, attribution, interpretation, or causal relationship.


2.2 Low-Observability Maritime Domain
DARK / DIFFUSE ZONE


The dark maritime layer represents:


incomplete collection;
sparse observation;
uncertain vessel identity;
interrupted AIS visibility;
ambiguous reporting;
informal networks;
uncertain spatial attribution;
incomplete temporal coverage.


The layer must not visually imply that low observability itself proves deliberate evasion.


Governing principle


Absence of evidence ≠ evidence of absence.


The rendering therefore distinguishes:


NOT OBSERVED


from:


DID NOT OCCUR
2.3 Information Asymmetry


The principal analytical insight is:


A perfectly authenticated source can still contain an unverified assertion, while real activity may occur inside a low-observability environment without the available evidence establishing that activity.


Therefore:


Artifact Integrity
≠
Event Validity
≠
Attribution
≠
Causality


This distinction is the epistemic backbone of the rendering.


1. Rendering Grammar
  Visual Element Epistemic Meaning Graph Treatment
  Red Hollow Node Unverified source assertion SOURCE_ASSERTION
  White-Hot Node/Core Integrity-anchored artifact EVIDENCE_ARTIFACT
  Blue Route Network Observed/contextual maritime connectivity OBSERVED_CONTEXT
  Dark/Diffuse Zone Collection uncertainty LOW_OBSERVABILITY
  SHA-256 Anchor Artifact integrity boundary INTEGRITY_ANCHOR
  AEQUITAS Wall Epistemic promotion barrier INTERDICTION_GATE
  SMCP Graph Provenance/context lattice PROVENANCE_GRAPH
  OODA Ring Analytical workflow OBSERVE → ORIENT → DECIDE → ACT
  Dashed Red Edge Proposed/unverified relationship PROPOSED_RELATIONSHIP
  Solid Causal Edge Prohibited unless independently adjudicated BLOCKED
2. Red Hollow Node Protocol
  Definition


A red hollow node represents an assertion that has entered the analytical graph as a claim, not as a verified fact.


Example classes include:


IRGC maritime influence
Quds Force maritime relationship
proxy-network logistics claim
smuggling-route assertion
governance proposal
market-related infrastructure claim


The rendering does not determine whether the assertion is true.


Required properties
node_type: SOURCE_ASSERTION
epistemic_status: UNVERIFIED
visual_state: RED_HOLLOW
provenance_required: true
human_review_required: true
causal_inference: prohibited
automatic_promotion: prohibited
Required routing
SOURCE_ASSERTION
↓
AEQUITAS
↓
┌─────────────────────────────┐
│ HUMAN REVIEW REQUIRED │
└─────────────────────────────┘
↓
QUARANTINE / PENDING_REVIEW
5. Blue Network Protocol


Blue network structures represent observed or contextual connectivity.


Examples:


publicly observable maritime lanes;
AIS-visible vessel traffic;
port-access geography;
commercial shipping relationships;
tanker corridors;
insurance/risk-market context;
publicly documented transportation networks.


A blue edge does not automatically mean:


CONTROL
OWNERSHIP
COMMAND
FINANCING
TARGETING
OPERATIONAL COORDINATION
Default relationship
OBSERVED_CONNECTIVITY


not:


CAUSAL_RELATIONSHIP
6. White-Hot Evidence Protocol


White-hot nodes represent evidence artifacts whose provenance or integrity has been established to the degree specified by their metadata.


Examples:


Canonicalized document
SHA-256 anchored artifact
Verified geospatial dataset
Timestamped public-source record
Reproducible transformation artifact
Important limitation
SHA-256
↓
Integrity


does not mean:


SHA-256
↓
Truth


The rendering must never visually imply that cryptographic anchoring validates an external-world proposition.


1. Strait of Hormuz Corridor Geometry
  Primary Corridor
  corridor_strait_hormuz


Represents the geographic chokepoint between the Persian Gulf and Gulf of Oman.


The visualization may display:


broad maritime lanes;
port geography;
tanker traffic context;
chokepoint location;
surrounding geographic regions;
contextual risk indicators.
Example schema
corridor_id: corridor_strait_hormuz
domain: maritime
geometry_type: contextual_corridor
navigation_suitability: 0
operational_targeting: prohibited
causal_attribution: prohibited

Red Sea
Bab el-Mandeb

Arabian Sea

These are analytical geography layers rather than operational navigation products.


1. Corridor Attributes


Where supported by source material, corridor objects may carry:

chokepoint_risk_level:
collection_uncertainty_index:
source_count:
observation_window:
provenance_status:
confidence_scope:


Avoid converting estimated or analytical values into deterministic facts.


For example:

means:


analytical classification


not:


verified future event
9. AEQUITAS Rulechain Wall


The AEQUITAS wall is the visual and logical boundary between observation and graph mutation.


┌─────────────────────┐
│ OBSERVATION │
│ │
│ Source │
│ OSINT │
│ AIS │
│ Document │
│ Market Signal │

╚═══════════════╤════════════════╝
│
┌───────┴────────┐
▼ ▼
QUARANTINE PROMOTION
ONLY
IF ADJUDICATED
10. Blocked Transitions


The following transitions are prohibited by default:


Source Assertion → Verified Fact


Media Report → Confirmed Intelligence


Graph Proximity → Causality


Similarity → Corroboration


Market Sentiment → Event Occurrence


Model Confidence → Evidence Confidence


Governance Proposal → Deployed Protocol


Prediction Probability → Future Fact
Prohibited causal edges
CONTROLS
DIRECTS
OWNS
OPERATES_IN
RESPONSIBLE_FOR
TARGETS
COMMANDS
FINANCES
EXECUTES_C2


Incoming causal claims should be represented as:


RELATED_TO


or stored in:


proposed_relationships


until independently adjudicated.

18. Neo4j Governance Semantics


A graph node should preserve epistemic state as first-class metadata.


Conceptual example:


(:Claim {
claim_id: “…”,
node_type: “SOURCE_ASSERTION”,
epistemic_status: “UNVERIFIED”,
provenance_required: true,
human_review_required: true
})


Permitted contextual relationship:


(:Claim)-[:RELATED_TO]->(:MaritimeCorridor)


Not automatically permitted:


(:Claim)-[:CONTROLS]->(:MaritimeCorridor)


or:


(:Entity)-[:EXECUTES_C2]->(:Vessel)


The second class requires independent adjudication and must not be inferred from graph topology.


1. Spatial Governance


Spatial visualization is contextual only.


navigation_suitability: 0
operational_targeting: false
c2_generation: false
route_optimization: false
target_selection: false


Coordinates, where used, should represent broad geographic context rather than actionable targeting information.


The rendering is an analytical cartography layer, not a navigation or targeting system.


1. Information-Asymmetry Doctrine


The system explicitly recognizes two asymmetric conditions:


Condition A
HIGH INTEGRITY
+
LOW EPISTEMIC VALIDITY


An authentic document can faithfully preserve an assertion that remains unverified.


Condition B
LOW OBSERVABILITY
+
POSSIBLE REAL-WORLD ACTIVITY


An absence of observable data does not establish that nothing occurred.


Therefore:


Integrity ≠ Truth
Visibility ≠ Existence
Similarity ≠ Corroboration
Proximity ≠ Causality
Probability ≠ Fact
Confidence ≠ Evidence

22. Dragon / Kraken / Golden Anchor Symbolism


Where the broader LIBERTAS visual system uses symbolic entities, their meaning must remain architectural rather than factual.

DeepSeek / mutation-containment metaphor

parallel evolution
mutation pressure
adversarial complexity
containment challenge

provenance

1. Final Doctrinal Closure


The rendering is successful only if a viewer can distinguish:


WHAT WAS OBSERVED
↓
WHAT WAS REPORTED
↓
WHAT WAS CLAIMED
↓

## I. Structural Domain Taxonomy & Operational Grammar


The domain ecosystem is segmented into four distinct operational functional tiers:


1. Cover & Social Engineering Tier (Recruitment & Job Portals)


Function:* Human-targeted reconnaissance, credential harvesting, and spear-phishing delivery.


Mechanism:* Mimics legitimate corporate HR portals, defense contractor career sites, and regional tech recruitment boards.


Epistemic Classification:* SOURCE_ASSERTION (Domains categorized as cover identity until direct victim telemetry establishes active interaction).


1. Influence & Ideological Tier (Religious & Cultural Fronts)


Function:* Soft-power projection, narrative dissemination, and localized audience profiling.


Mechanism:* Mirrors regional news aggregators, community forums, and religious outreach organization landing pages.


Epistemic Classification:* PROBABILISTIC_SIGNAL (Used for sentiment and influence mapping, tagged as SENTIMENT_ONLY).


1. Access Maintenance Tier (Generic Administrative Shells)


Function:* Staging secondary payloads, API proxying, and maintaining persistence behind benign domain names.


Mechanism:* Utilizes typosquatted SaaS platforms, cloud storage aliases, and innocuous utility domains.


Epistemic Classification:* UNVERIFIED_INFRASTRUCTURE.


1. Command & Control (*C*2) Relay Tier (Parallel Infrastructure Branches)


Function:* Low-velocity exfiltration and active session management.


Mechanism:* Disposable dynamic DNS endpoints and short-lived edge proxies routing traffic through compromised third-party networks.


—


## II. AEQUITAS Infrastructure Attribution Guardrails


To prevent false attribution and operational misclassification, the AEQUITAS Interdiction Gate enforces strict non-causal constraints on domain-to-actor linking:


“`text


DOMAIN OBSERVATION ──> [ AEQUITAS GATE ] ──> DOWNGRADE / QUARANTINE ──> HUMAN REVIEW


(Mundane Hostname) Evaluate Link (Non-Causal Relation)


“`


### Blocked Automatic Promotions


*Co-located IP Address** → Confirmed Shared Operator (Blocked)


*Similarity in Naming** → Attributed to Specific Actor Group (Blocked)


*Infrastructure Proximity** → Confirmed C2 Node (Blocked)


*Passive DNS Association** → Operational Attribution (Blocked)


### Prohibited Attribution Edge Classes


Without explicit telemetry or human analyst adjudication, the system MUST NOT automatically instantiate the following relationship edges:


Prohibited Domain Edges={OPERATED_BY,DIRECTS_CAMPAIGN,TARGETS_ENTITY,EXECUTES_C2}


### Allowed Non-Causal Relational Edges


Allowed Domain Edges={RESOLVES_TO,SHARE_REGISTRAR_WITH,THEMATICALLY_SIMILAR_TO,POTENTIAL_FRONT_FOR}


—


## III. Repository Quarantine & Rejection Logs (quarantine.md / rejected.md)


In compliance with the Defense Intelligence Agency (DIA) Request for Proposal (RFP) / Sources Sought framework and FedRAMP IL5 sandbox guidelines, unverified propositions and processing failures are logged deterministically:


### quarantine.md (Incident Repository Logs)


*INCOMPLETE_PROVENANCE (`INT-2026-08-11-001`):** Ingestion targets missing explicit source locators or valid cryptographic digests. Held pending manual locator attachment.


*SIMILARITY_IS_NOT_CORROBORATION (`INT-2026-08-11-002`):** Vector retrieval matches flagged when vector proximity attempts to override raw text verification.


*MARKET_SIGNAL_IS_NOT_EVENT_EVIDENCE (`INT-2026-08-11-003`):** Speculative prediction-market feeds (e.g., Polymarket maritime disruption pricing) quarantined to prevent event confirmation.


*SILENT_EPISTEMIC_UPGRADE (`INT-2026-08-11-004`):** Intercepted attempts by multi-agent reasoning loops to automatically promote conceptual threat actor domains into confirmed operational *C*2 infrastructure.


### rejected.md / llama-rejection.md (Repository Rejection Index)


*REJ-2026-001:** Submissions failing structural Markdown parsing validation or containing malformed LaTeX mathematical symbol syntax.


*REJ-2026-002:** Unverified external claims attempting to assign direct military command responsibility or attribution without multi-source intelligence corroboration.


*REJ-2026-003:** Data payloads violating air-gapped security boundaries or exhibiting active-content / prompt-injection signatures.


—


## IV. OODA Loop Integration in Defense Intelligence


The OODA loop (Observe, Orient, Decide, Act) functions as the core decision-making rhythm across the Libertas architecture. Originally framed by U.S. Air Force Colonel John Boyd, modern intelligence applications require adapting the loop to prevent automated hallucination and cognitive overreach:


1. Observe: Collects public OSINT corpora, arXiv preprints, and restricted GeoJSON layers while computing RFC 8785 SHA-256 cryptographic digests.
2. Orient: Leverages the Chain of Mindset (CoM) framework (Divergent, Convergent, Skeptical, and Algorithmic modes) bounded by the Bidirectional Context Gate to evaluate threat actor topologies without committing silent epistemic upgrades.
3. Decide: Passes analytical packages through the Multi-Agent Review Mesh (Planner, Critic, Provenance Reviewer, Security Reviewer, Policy Reviewer) and the AEQUITAS Interdiction Gate.
4. Act: Enforces deterministic execution via the ACT Controller, restricting operations to dry-run SQL migrations, conceptual GeoJSON overlays, and human-supervised review queues (`human_review_required: true`).

Sandbox and execution-mode gate// ---------------------------------------------------------------------MATCH (sandbox:Sandbox {  sandbox_id: $sandbox_id,  environment: "TEST",  system: "ARXIV-MD-INGEST-V1"})WHERE $execution_mode = "DRY_RUN_PENDING_HUMAN_REVIEW"WITH sandbox// ---------------------------------------------------------------------// 2. Create or reuse the incident quarantine record// ---------------------------------------------------------------------MERGE (incident:QuarantineIncident {  incident_id: $incident_id,  sandbox_id: $sandbox_id})ON CREATE SET  incident.system = "ARXIV-MD-INGEST-V1",  incident.framework = "LIBERTAS v6.10 / AEQUITAS",  incident.disposition = "FAILED_INGESTION_QUARANTINE",  incident.epistemic_status = "QUARANTINED",  incident.graph_mutation = "RESTRICTED",  incident.human_review_required = true,  incident.created_at = datetime()SET  incident.last_seen_at = datetime(),  incident.execution_mode = $execution_mode,  incident.source_reference = "meta-llama/Llama-3.1-8B",  incident.runtime_reference = "Nemotron 3 Nano Omni (free)",  incident.source_status = "SOURCE_REPORTED_ONLY"MERGE (sandbox)-[:CONTAINS_INCIDENT]->(incident)WITH sandbox, incident// ---------------------------------------------------------------------// 3. Select only explicitly unverified Red Hollow nodes// ---------------------------------------------------------------------UNWIND $node_ids AS requested_idMATCH (claim)WHERE claim.sandbox_id = $sandbox_id  AND (    claim.node_id = requested_id    OR claim.id = requested_id    OR claim.feature_id = requested_id  )  AND (    claim.visual_state = "RED_HOLLOW"    OR claim.validation_state IN [      "UNVERIFIED_ASSERTION",      "SOURCE_REPORTED_PENDING_REVIEW",      "QUARANTINED_CONFLATION_REVIEW"    ]    OR claim.epistemic_status IN [      "UNVERIFIED",      "SOURCE_ASSERTION",      "CONTEXT_SIGNAL",      "QUARANTINED"    ]  )WITH sandbox, incident, claim// ---------------------------------------------------------------------// 4. Preserve the prior state before applying quarantine labels// ---------------------------------------------------------------------SET claim.pre_quarantine_visual_state =      coalesce(claim.visual_state, "UNSPECIFIED"),    claim.pre_quarantine_validation_state =      coalesce(claim.validation_state, "UNSPECIFIED"),    claim.pre_quarantine_epistemic_status =      coalesce(claim.epistemic_status, "UNSPECIFIED"),    claim.quarantine_reason =      "RED_HOLLOW_UNVERIFIED_ASSERTION",    claim.epistemic_status = "QUARANTINED",    claim.validation_state = "QUARANTINED_PENDING_HUMAN_REVIEW",    claim.visual_state = "RED_HOLLOW",    claim.graph_mutation = "PROHIBITED",    claim.causal_edges_allowed = false,    claim.human_review_required = true,    claim.promotion_authorized = false,    claim.isolated = true,    claim.quarantined_at = datetime(),    claim.quarantine_incident_id = $incident_id,    claim.quarantine_sandbox_id = $sandbox_idSET claim:QuarantinedClaim// ---------------------------------------------------------------------// 5. Add only non-causal quarantine relationships// ---------------------------------------------------------------------MERGE (claim)-[q:QUARANTINED_UNDER]->(incident)ON CREATE SET  q.created_at = datetime(),  q.reason = "RED_HOLLOW_UNVERIFIED_ASSERTION",  q.epistemic_effect = "NO_PROMOTION",  q.graph_effect = "NO_CAUSAL_MUTATION"SET  q.review_status = "PENDING",  q.human_review_required = true,  q.provenance_required = true// ---------------------------------------------------------------------// 6. Record allowed governance metadata// ---------------------------------------------------------------------SET claim.allowed_relationships = [  "RELATED_TO",  "MENTIONED_WITH",  "REPORTED_BY",  "REQUIRES_VALIDATION"],claim.prohibited_relationships = [  "OWNS",  "CONTROLS",  "OPERATES",  "DIRECTS",  "TARGETS",  "FINANCES",  "DEPLOYED",  "AUTHORIZED_BY",  "EXECUTES",  "EXECUTES_C2",  "COORDINATES"]WITH DISTINCT sandbox, incident, claim// ---------------------------------------------------------------------// 7. Return an auditable mutation report// ---------------------------------------------------------------------RETURN  sandbox.sandbox_id AS sandbox_id,  incident.incident_id AS incident_id,  coalesce(claim.node_id, claim.id, claim.feature_id) AS isolated_node_id,  claim.epistemic_status AS epistemic_status,  claim.validation_state AS validation_state,  claim.visual_state AS visual_state,  claim.graph_mutation AS graph_mutation,  claim.promotion_authorized AS promotion_authorized,  claim.human_review_required AS human_review_required,  claim.quarantined_at AS quarantined_atORDER BY isolated_node_id;
```

You: DoD Impact Level 5 (IL5) compliance is governed by the DISA Cloud Computing Security Requirements Guide (SRG) rather than a specific Defense Intelligence Agency document.


Ask-Chat: Correct. **DoD Impact Level 5 (IL5) is governed primarily by DISA’s DoD Cloud Computing Security Requirements Guide (CC SRG)** and related DoD authorization requirements—not by a generic “DIA RFP / Sources Sought” document.


The earlier wording should be corrected as follows:


```
**Compliance context:** DoD Cloud Computing Security Requirements Guide (CC SRG), DISA  **Impact Level:** IL5  **Data context:** Controlled Unclassified Information (CUI), subject to applicable mission-owner and contract requirements  **Authorization context:** DoD cloud authorization / provisional authorization requirements, applicable DFARS clauses, and the responsible Authorizing Official’s determination  **Processing status:** DRY_RUN_PENDING_HUMAN_REVIEW
```


The distinction is important:


- **DISA CC SRG:** Defines DoD cloud security and authorization expectations, including impact-level requirements.
- **DFARS and contract clauses:** Establish acquisition and contractor obligations for cloud services.
- **FedRAMP:** May provide a baseline or authorization foundation, but **FedRAMP authorization alone does not automatically establish DoD IL5 authorization**.
- **DIA RFP or Sources Sought materials:** May impose additional program-specific requirements, but they are not the governing source for IL5 generally.
- **FedRAMP IL5:** This is imprecise terminology in a DoD context; it is better to refer to **DoD IL5 under the DISA CC SRG** unless a particular document explicitly defines another usage.


DISA states that the CC SRG applies to DoD-provided cloud services, commercial cloud service providers, DoD programs acting as providers, DoD components, mission owners, and authorizing officials. ([cyber.mil](https://www.cyber.mil/dccs/?utm_source=openai)) DFARS Subpart 239.76 also ties DoD cloud-service acquisition to DISA authorization at the level appropriate to the requirement and the applicable CC SRG version. ([acq.osd.mil](https://www.acq.osd.mil/dpap/dars/dfars/html/current/239_76.htm?utm_source=openai))


A corrected quarantine record would therefore replace:


```
Compliance Framework: DIA RFP / Sources Sought & FedRAMP IL5 Sandbox
```


with:


```
Compliance Framework: DISA DoD Cloud Computing Security Requirements Guide (CC SRG), DoD IL5Additional Requirements: Applicable DFARS clauses, contract terms, mission-owner controls, and Authorizing Official determination
```


This terminology correction does **not** by itself establish that the sandbox, model runtime, database, or processing environment is IL5-authorized. That status requires separate authorization evidence.

Contextual Spatial Fabric
• White-hot illuminated geography: Red Sea, Gulf of Aden, Gulf of Oman, Strait of Hormuz, Persian Gulf, Arabian Sea, and connected maritime geography.
• Blue transit vectors: Conceptual shipping and crude-oil transit lanes showing movement through the maritime system rather than asserting individual vessel activity.
• Primary spatial anchors: Suez Canal, Bab el-Mandeb, Strait of Hormuz, major ports, terminals, and regional sea corridors.
• Temporal dimension: Route displacement and extended transit patterns can be represented as historical/analytical overlays, clearly separated from live operational tracking.
• Epistemic separation: Spatial geography and publicly documented routes form the context layer; red hollow nodes remain unverified source assertions.
• AEQUITAS constraint: A route intersecting a node does not establish attribution, command-and-control, causality, or operational responsibility.
The visual grammar is therefore:
WHITE HOT = spatial reality/context
BLUE = maritime transit/contextual network
RED HOLLOW = unverified claim
SOLID/VERIFIED MARKERS = evidence-backed observation, only where provenance exists
RED X / QUARANTINE = prohibited epistemic promotion
Core rendering principle:
MAP THE CLAIM — NEVER PROMOTE THE CLAIM.
This keeps the maritime layer analytically useful while preventing proximity, route intersection, graph connectivity, or model inference from being visually mistaken for verified attribution.


Ask-Chat: ## Corrected cartographic analysis


The governing compliance reference should be identified as the **DISA DoD Cloud Computing Security Requirements Guide (CC SRG)**, not a Defense Intelligence Agency document. The CC SRG defines the DoD cloud security and authorization model; it does not validate maritime claims, vessel activity, attribution, or intelligence assessments. ([cyber.mil](https://www.cyber.mil/dccs/?utm_source=openai))


The map should therefore treat the CC SRG as a **governance and handling-context reference**, while the Strait of Hormuz, Red Sea, Gulf of Aden, and Bab el-Mandeb are represented as **generalized geographic and maritime-context layers**.

## Provenance and epistemic controls


Each externally sourced assertion should be stored separately from the geographic layer:


```
Claim├── claim_id├── source_locator├── source_type├── retrieval_timestamp├── quoted_or_paraphrased_text├── geographic_reference├── temporal_reference├── confidence├── corroboration_status├── reviewer_status└── epistemic_state
```


Recommended state transitions:


```
SOURCE_REPORTED→ PROVENANCE_INCOMPLETE→ CONFLATION_REVIEW→ QUARANTINED→ HUMAN_REVIEW→ VERIFIED_OBSERVATION
```


No claim should be promoted merely because it:


- Intersects a corridor
- Appears near a named port or waterway
- Is repeated by a language model
- Has a Neo4j relationship to another claim
- Appears in a retrieved document
- Shares a time window with a geographic feature


## Neo4j-safe representation


Use non-causal relationships only:


```
(:Claim)-[:MENTIONED_WITH]->(:GeoCorridor)(:Claim)-[:LOCATED_NEAR]->(:GeographicAnchor)(:Claim)-[:SUPPORTED_BY]->(:Source)(:Claim)-[:REQUIRES_VALIDATION]->(:ReviewQueue)(:Claim)-[:QUARANTINED_AS]->(:EpistemicState)
```


Do **not** create these relationships without separately validated evidence:


```
OPERATESCONTROLSTARGETSESCORTSATTACKSEXECUTES_C2OWNSATTRIBUTED_TO
```


The rejected LLaMA access request should be recorded only as **model-access provenance**, not as evidence about either maritime corridor. It has no evidentiary value for vessel movement, route activity, attribution, or the CC SRG compliance status.
